We can implement a robust local optimizer using exact FFT objective and subgradient with active peaks. Let's design in detail.

Objective calculation:
```
def conv_full(a):
    n=a.size
    size=2*n-1
    fft_len=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, fft_len)[:size]
    # correct small negative
    c=np.maximum(c,0)
    return c
def objective(c,n): 2*n*np.max(c)
```
Given normalized sum=1. We can use double precision.

Pseudo for `mirror_step(a, temp, top_frac, weights_power, eta_list)`:

2. Determine active indices: idx = np.where(c > M*(1 - frac))[0] maybe plus top_k. For uniform, top band includes near-center, gradient nonconstant near edges. Good.
3. Weights: For active c, maybe `w = (c / M)^power`, normalized. Power 10 makes near-max high. But if only center much higher than shoulders, center weight dominates; for uniform center slightly higher than shoulders (n vs n-1), power maybe not too high. We can use power 2-4.
4. Compute grad: initialize zeros. For each active m with weight w_m: `g += w_m * 2 * a[slice]` with orientation? For c_m, derivative =2 ∑ a_j a_{m-j}. For fixed m, vector over j: a[m-j] for j valid. 5. center_grad = g - g.mean() (on all n). Now log factor = -eta*center_grad. To avoid overflow, subtract max. `new_a = a*np.exp(logf)`. If any new_a zero, floor. Normalize.
6. To line search, we can precompute for a base `s = -center_grad` (direction). For η values, new = normalize(a*exp(η*s)). Because s can be large. Choose η relative to `max abs s`. Try η = [1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2,0.1,0.3,1,3,10,30,100] times maybe 1/std. Actually s can have values O(1/n)? g ~M/n? For n=128, a~1/n, g~1/n=0.008, s std~0.004. η up to1000 needed? Objective and gradient:
```
def full_score(a):
    n = len(a)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < eps: return inf
    a = a/s
    c = fft_conv(a)
    return 2*n*c.max(), a
```

Subgradient for weights w over c:
```
def gradient_for_weights(a, w):
    n=len(a)
    if w.size != 2*n-1: ...
    g = np.zeros(n)
    # only active where w>0
    idx = np.nonzero(w)[0]
    for m_int in idx:
        m=int(m_int); wm=w[m_int]
        if wm == 0: continue
        j0=max(0, m-n+1); j1=min(n-1, m)
        # a[m-j] for j in [j0,j1]
        # start index for a: m-j1, end m-j0
        g[j0:j1+1] += wm * a[m-j1 : m-j0+1][::-1]
    return g
```
Factor maybe 1. Note for m=0, j1=0, a[0]; g0 +=w0*a0; derivative would 2w0*a0; factor constant if we use all m? For each term actually derivative 2 w_m a_{m-j}. Thus g should be doubled. But if we always multiply by 2, scale. Use factor 2.

But if we use softmax weights normalized sum=1, g magnitude ~2/n. So scale by n/2 maybe. We'll compute `g *= n` to O(1).

Update direction: Since we are on simplex, subtract mean of g (or projected gradient). But if using log-multiplicative, `u = g - g.mean()`. We want decrease objective; for max function at peaks, active g positive. So direction = -u. For variables with high involvement, decreased; others increased. This may raise some other peaks; line search.

Temperature/weights: We need choose w. Let c, M. We can use:
- `base = np.clip(c / M - 0.5, 0, None)`; w = base**power; if sum zero, take top_k.
- Or `w = np.exp((c - M)*beta/M)` for c > M*(1 - delta). Choose beta 10-100. But if M small, numerical.
- For robust, combine: top 5 indices always get some weight plus softmax band. The top may be one cluster; include indices sorted by c descending for count `active_count` = max(3, int(0.02*len(c)))? For n=128, 5; n=1024,20. Weight by ranks. This prevents center-only if only one active? But top 5 consecutive maybe near peak; for uniform top5 includes center and neighbors, gradient nonconstant. Pseudo weights:
```
def active_weights(c, M, frac=0.05, power=2.0):
    L=len(c)
    thresh = M*(1.0 - frac)   # include peaks within 5% of max
    active = c > thresh
    if active.sum() < 3:
        # take top 3
        idx=np.argsort(c)[-3:]; active[idx]=True
    w = np.zeros(L)
    wvals = c[active]/M
    w[active]=wvals**power
    # Additionally ensure global max has weight? If sum zero
    if w.sum()==0: ...
    w/=w.sum()
    return w
```
frac maybe 0.1, power 4-16. Temperature schedule: start low power (broad), then increase as improve? Use parameter `focus` from 1 to 50.

Step size schedule:
- Compute `u = g - np.mean(g)` (after g scaled). Let `scale = np.linalg.norm(u)` (or maxabs). We can choose base step `eta = 1.0 / (scale + 1e-8)`? Because exp(-eta*u) with eta ~1/scale gives O(1). Try `etas = base * [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1,3,10]`. But if u has mean zero, maxabs maybe ~1. For n=1024, g scaled by n maybe O1. Use base=1.0 maybe. We can line search.
- For each eta, candidate: `log_factor = -eta*u`; for stability subtract log_factor.max? If eta positive and u range, log_factor can large. Use `lf = -eta*u; lf -= lf.max(); cand = a*exp(lf)`. This scales candidate but normalization removes. If a has tiny values and log_factor negative large, underflow to zero; okay. Then normalize. If cand sum < eps, skip.
- Evaluate score. Choose eta with best score. If best score >= current - 1e-12, try even smaller steps or perturb weights. To avoid too many, maybe 5-7 etas.
- After choosing, if improved, update best local; set `lr_factor` maybe increase if success else decrease.

But line search per step with 7 FFTs for n=2048 maybe okay? 7* maybe 0.005s=0.035s; 1000 steps 35s. Fine. Use no evaluate_sequence until end.

Convergence: Mirror descent may be slow. We can perform multiple iterations with same weights? We can recompute c each iteration. If no improvement, change weights: reduce frac (narrower), increase power, random perturb, or initialize from previous plus noise.

Use a "checkpoint" after each outer iteration re-evaluate best. Keep `history_no_improve`; if >50, adjust parameters:
   - Increase focus/power: power *=3 (max100), frac*=0.5.
   - Or random restart from best with small multiplicative noise.
   - Or run a "annealing" step accepting a small worsening (e.g. 1e-4) to escape.

LP algorithm outline:
```
def lp_direction(a, time_limit=None):
  n=len(a); L=2*n-1
  A = sparse (L, n+1)
  for m in 0..L-1:
    j0...j1; A[m, j0:j1+1] = a[j?]? Need row for y_j = a_{m-j}; so coefficients for y_j are a[m-j]. For j valid, coeff = a[m-j]. Last col -1.
  Run linprog(c=[0..,1], A_ub=A, b_ub=zeros(L), A_eq=ones(1,n+1) with last0, b_eq=1, bounds y(0,n)[:,(None,0, None)] and t (0,None) maybe M)
  Return y, t
```

Implement `optimize_sequence` combining mirror and LP:
```
def optimize_sequence(a0, time_limit, use_lp=True, mirror_first=True):
    a = normalize(a0)
    best_a=a; best_score=score_of(a)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    power=2.0; frac=0.1; no_improve=0; lp_count=0
    while time-left > 2:
        if use_lp and (no_improve > 10 or every 50): attempt lp update; if improve, reset
        else: mirror iteration with line search.
        if no improvement > 30: adjust power*=2, frac*=0.6, or random perturb best and restart local.
    return best
```

```

Function `get_lp_base(n)`:
```
L=2*n-1
rows=[]; cols=[]; cidx=[]
for m in range(L):
  j0=max(0,m-n+1); j1=min(n-1,m)
  for j in range(j0,j1+1):
     rows.append(m); cols.append(j); cidx.append(m-j)
return np.array(rows,int64), np.array(cols,int64), np.array(cidx,int32)
```
Total loops n^2; for n=1024 1M build maybe 0.1s. Cache per n.
Then `A_y = csr_matrix((a[cidx], (rows, cols)), shape=(L,n))`.

Potential problem: Multiplicative update and line search may get stuck at local minima with objective ~1.52. LP can reduce. We can implement an outer "best improvement" over multiple directions:
- For each top candidate, generate a set of directions: (a) mirror top peaks, (b) LP, (c) coordinate flips, (d) perturbations. Choose best neighbor.
- This is like stochastic local search. But mirror line search already uses subgradient.

Could use "crossover": Given current a, LP y. Instead of just mixing, try y directly. Self-conv of y maybe high, but LP ensures cross low. Direct y might be part of solution; blend with varying λ. Good.

Could solve the LP repeatedly and iterate to fixed point with λ=1 (a=y). This is like alternating: a ← y(a) where y minimizes max a*y. Does sequence converge to a point where self conv max = cross min? Let's implement an outer loop with λ=1 if improves, else mix.

LP can produce a sparse y with tiny values; direct y may have huge self-conv if sparse. But if LP minimizes max a*y, y might be broad enough. We'll test via blending.

Another powerful approach: Because LP is linear, we can find g that minimizes maximum of a*g; then the update g is in a "dual feasible" set. This resembles "Sion's minimax"? Could provide monotonic decrease if choose λ appropriately? Let's see: For current a, t*=max(a*g*) ≤M. Consider b=(1-λ)a+λg. Its self-conv max = (1-λ)^2M_a +2λ(1-λ)T + λ^2 M_g. T≤M_a. If M_g is not too high, for small λ derivative at 0: 2λ(T-M_a) negative if T<M_a. So local decrease if t* <M_a. If t*=M_a, derivative maybe 0. In that case we need directional second order or alternative. If LP returns t*<M, good. If t*==M but g≠a, maybe still improvement for some λ? Derivative 0, second order depends. We can line search λ to improve.

We can also use LP with a slightly relaxed upper bound (1+eps)M and maximize sum to find g with small t? Actually min t is best. If t* close M, mirror descent may handle.

Let's implement LP line search λ: if improvement, accept best λ; update a. Repeat LP/mirror.

`update_best(a)`:
```
def update_best(a):
    global BEST_SCORE, BEST_SEQ
    if a is None: return
    try:
        aa=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
        if aa.ndim!=1 or aa.size==0: return
        aa=np.clip(aa,0,1000.0)
        s=aa.sum()
        if s<1e-12: return
        aa=aa/s
        sc=score_array(aa)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < BEST_SCORE - 1e-12:
            BEST_SCORE=sc; BEST_SEQ=aa.copy()
    except: pass
```
But `score_array` uses FFT. If we update many times in line search, maybe compute score and call? We'll directly in optimize return. But global update for initial.

`score_array(a)`:
```
def score_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-12: return inf
    a=a/s
    n=a.size
    c=fft_conv(a)
    M=c.max()
    return float(2.0*n*M)
```
FFT conv:
```
def fft_conv(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    size=2*n-1
    # pad to power of 2 for speed
    m=1
    while m<size: m <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,m)[:size]
    c=np.real(c)
    # small negatives
    c[c<0]=0
    return c
```
Using while loop in function; allowed? It's not nested closure. Could use `1 << (size-1).bit_length()`.

`make_weights(c, focus_power, frac, min_active)` top-level:
```
def make_weights(c, focus_power=4.0, frac=0.10, min_active=5):
    M=float(c.max())
    if M <=0: return np.zeros_like(c)
    L=len(c)
    w=np.zeros(L)
    thresh=M*(1.0-frac)
    vals=c > thresh
    if vals.sum() < min_active:
        idx=np.argsort(c)[-min_active:]
        vals[idx]=True
    # For stability, don't include extremely small compared to max? If frac large, okay.
    x=c[vals]/M
    # Avoid zeros? c nonnegative; weights for zero if min_active includes low peaks. Use x = np.maximum(x, 1e-6)
    x=np.maximum(x,1e-6)
    w[vals]=x**focus_power
    sw=w.sum()
    if sw <=0:
        w[np.argmax(c)]=1
    else: w/=sw
    return w
```
For uniform, c max center, next 1-1/n; frac .1 includes peaks >.9M around center ~? triangular width maybe small. min_active ensures at least 5.

`gradient_from_weights(a,w)`:
```
def gradient_from_weights(a,w):
    n=a.size
    g=np.zeros(n)
    thresh=w.max()*1e-10
    for m_int in np.nonzero(w > thresh)[0]:
        m=int(m_int); wm=w[m_int]
        if wm ==0: continue
        j0=0 if m>=n-1 else m-(n-1)
        j1=m if m<n else n-1
        # if j0 > j1 continue
        # Slice a[m-j1 : m-j0+1][::-1]
        start=m - j1
        end=m - j0
        seg=a[start:end+1][::-1]
        g[j0:j1+1] += wm * seg
    return g
```
Check for m=0: j0=0,j1=0,start=0,end=0 seg=a[0]. ok. m=n-1: j0=0,j1=n-1,start=0? m-j1 = n-1-(n-1)=0, end=n-1, seg=a[0:n][::-1] = reversed a, g[j]=a[m-j]=a[n-1-j], correct. m=2n-2: j0=n-1,j1=n-1, start=2n-2-(n-1)=n-1,end same.

Scale gradient: `g *= float(n) / max(1.0, np.max(g) if g.size else 1.0)`? Since g ~O(1/n) if weights sum1 and a~1/n. For peak single, max g ~max a ~maybe 1/√n? If a has edge high, max g maybe >1/n. Use `scale = max(np.max(g), 1e-12)`; multiply by `1.0/scale` to make max 1, instead of n. The step base then O(1). But if all a uniform, g max=1/n, scale small, base =1/(1/n)=n, too large? Actually if scale=1/n, base=n, etas base*0.1 =0.2n=128 for n128, log factor huge. We want base maybe 1. We can do `u = g - mean(g); u_max = np.abs(u).max(); base = 1.0/(u_max + 1e-8)` could be large but that's okay? If u_max ~1/n, base ~n, eta list up to 10n -> log factor ~10, too big. But candidate normalized; maybe too aggressive. We can cap base at e.g. 20. Or scale g to have standard deviation 1: `g -= mean; u_std = g.std(); if u_std>0: g/=u_std`. Then etas absolute [0.01..10]. This is stable.
So:
```
g = gradient...*n? Maybe don't multiply.
mean=np.mean(g)
g=g-mean
std=np.std(g)
if std > 1e-12: g = g / std
# direction -g
```
Then `lf = -eta*g`, eta list [0.001,0.003,0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1,3,10]. This explores. But if original gradient has large asymmetry, scaling by std removes scale; step size chosen by line search. Good. The factor 2 in gradient constant irrelevant.

LP step:
```
def lp_candidate(a):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
    n=a.size
    if n<64 or n>1024: return None? We can allow 2048 maybe.
    t=(time.monotonic())
    c=fft_conv(a); M=c.max()
    if M <=0: return None
    rows, cols, cidx = get_lp_base(n)
    A_y = sparse.csr_matrix((a[cidx], (rows, cols)), shape=(2*n-1, n))
    L=2*n-1
    rows_t=np.arange(L); cols_t=rows_t; data_t=-np.ones(L)
    A_t = sparse.csr_matrix((data_t,(rows_t,cols_t)), shape=(L,1))
    A_ub = sparse.hstack((A_y, A_t), format='csc')
    b_ub=np.zeros(L)
    c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[-1]=1.0
    A_eq = sparse.lil_matrix((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1; A_eq=A_eq.tocsr()
    b_eq=np.ones(1)
    bounds=[(0,1.0)]*n + [(0.0, float(M)*(1+1e-8))]
    res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'disp': False})
    if res.status not in (0,3): return None
    y=np.asarray(res.x[:n],float)
    y=np.maximum(y,0)
    s=y.sum()
    if s<1e-8: return None
    y/=s
    # line search blends
    best=None; best_sc=score_array(a)
    for lam in (1.0, 0.8,0.6,0.4,0.2,0.1,0.05):
       cand=(1-lam)*a + lam*y
       cand=np.maximum(cand,0)
       cand/=cand.sum()
       sc=score_array(cand)
       if sc < best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=cand.copy()
    return best
```
Status 3 may be "unknown"? In scipy Highs status 0 optimal, 3 no convergence? Actually 3 unknown. Only accept 0. Also if res.x has nan skip.

`linprog` objective min; c_obj length n+1. Good.

Potential memory/time for sparse hstack each call. Could build A_ub directly triple: data = np.concatenate([a[cidx], -np.ones(L)]); rows all; cols all. But existing okay.

Now optimization loop details:
```
def optimize_sequence(a0, time_limit, allow_lp=True):
    a=normalize(a0)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=score_array(a)
    update_best(a)
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+time_limit
    power=3.0; frac=0.12; no=0; iter_count=0; last_lp=0
    accept_tol=1e-8
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
       iter_count+=1
       improved=False
       # occasionally LP
       if allow_lp and len(a)<=1024 and (iter_count == 1 or no >= 8 or iter_count % 40 == 0) and time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
           cand=lp_candidate(a)
           if cand is not None:
               sc=score_array(cand)
               if sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                   a=cand; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; update_best(a); no=0; improved=True
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2: break
       # mirror step
       cand, sc_cand, current = mirror_step(a, power, frac)
       if cand is not None and sc_cand < best_sc - 1e-10:
           a=cand; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc_cand; update_best(a); no=0; improved=True
       elif cand is not None and sc_cand < best_sc + 1e-6 and no > 20: # accept small for explore
           a=cand; # don't update best if not improve?
           no += 1? 
       else:
           no += 1
       if not improved:
           # adjust weights
           if no % 10 == 0:
              power = min(power*2.0, 200.0)
              frac = max(frac*0.7, 0.01)
           if no > 50:
              # random perturb best
              a=best_a.copy()
              rng=np.random.default_rng(int(time.monotonic()*1000)% (1<<31))
              noise = 1.0 + 0.2*rng.normal(size=a.size)
              a = np.maximum(a*noise, 1e-15)
              a -= a.mean()? no normalize
              a = a/ a.sum()
              # if score too bad, revert after maybe? We'll continue; best preserved.
              no=0
              power=2.0; frac=0.1
       # if best score very low break
       if best_sc < 1.0: break
    return best_a, best_sc
```
But after random perturb, `a` may be worse; next mirror line search from worse can still escape. We keep best separately. If time left, okay.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    global BEST_SCORE, BEST_SEQ, CAND_STORE

```
n_jobs = len(jobs)
time_per = max(20.0, (deadline - time.monotonic() - 60.0)/max(1,n_jobs))
for idx,(sc0,n,a) in enumerate(jobs):
    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30: break

Pruning:
```
def prune_results(results, max_total=40, max_per_len=4):
   results.sort(key=sort_key_result)
   jobs=[]; counts={}
   for item in results:
      sc,n,a=item
      if len(jobs)>=max_total: break
      c=counts.get(n,0)
      if c >= max_per_len: continue
      jobs.append((sc,n,a)); counts[n]=c+1
   # ensure global best? If best not included due per_len? The first overall best will be included.
   return jobs
```

```
def mirror_step_from_c(a, c, current_score, power, frac):
    w = make_weights(c, power, frac)
    g = gradient_from_weights(a,w)
    mean=np.mean(g); g-=mean
    std=np.std(g)
    if std < 1e-12:
       return None, current_score
    g/=std
    best_cand=None; best_sc=current_score
    for eta in ETA_STEPS:
       lf = -eta * g
       lf -= np.max(lf)
       cand = a * np.exp(lf)
       s = cand.sum()
       if s < 1e-12: continue
       cand = np.maximum(cand, 1.0e-15)
       cand /= cand.sum()
       sc = score_array(cand)
       if sc < best_sc:
          best_sc=sc; best_cand=cand.copy()
    return best_cand, best_sc
```

`lp_candidate(a)`:
```
def lp_candidate(a):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
    n=a.size
    if n < 32 or n > 512: return None
    c=fft_conv(a); M=c.max()
    if M <=0: return None
    rows, cols, cidx = get_lp_base(n)
    L=2*n-1
    A_y = sparse.csr_matrix((a[cidx], (rows, cols)), shape=(L,n), dtype=np.float64)
    # Add -t column via CSR:
    rows_t = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
    data_t = -np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)
    A_t = sparse.csr_matrix((data_t, (rows_t, rows_t)), shape=(L,1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_ub = sparse.hstack((A_y, A_t), format='csc')
    b_ub = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    c_obj = np.zeros(n+1, dtype=np.float64); c_obj[-1]=1.0
    A_eq = sparse.lil_matrix((1,n+1), dtype=np.float64); A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0; A_eq = A_eq.tocsr()
    b_eq = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    # y bounds 0..1 because sum1; t bounds 0..M
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)]*n
    bounds.append((0.0, float(M)*(1.0+1.0e-8)))
    res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'disp': False})
    if res is None or res.status != 0:
       return None
    y = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.maximum(y,0.0)
    s=y.sum()
    if s < 1e-8: return None
    y = y/s
    # line search
    best_cand = None; best_sc = score_array(a)
    # include exact y? For lambda list
    for lam in (1.0,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.15,0.05):
       cand = (1.0 - lam)*a + lam*y
       cand = np.maximum(cand,0.0)
       ss = cand.sum()
       if ss <=1e-12: continue
       cand /= ss
       sc = score_array(cand)
       if sc < best_sc:
          best_sc=sc; best_cand=cand.copy()
    return best_cand
```
If LP solution y=a and no improve, returns None. Good.

Time: `linprog` could take seconds. In optimize, call only when time left >20 and n<=512.

`normalize_array`:
```
def normalize_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12:
       return None
    a/=s
    return a
```

Pseudo:
```
def generate_arrays(n, rng, parent):
    out=[]
    if n <=0: return out
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = x / float(max(1, n-1))
    out.append(np.ones(n))
    exps = (0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0,1.25,1.5,2.0)
    for p in exps:
       a = (x+1.0)**p
       out.append(a)
       out.append(a[::-1].copy())
    betas=(0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0,4.0)
    for b in betas:
       a=np.exp(b*t)
       out.append(a)
       out.append(a[::-1].copy())
    for amp in (0.2,0.5,0.8):
      for ph in (0.0,0.7853981633974483,1.5707963267948966,2.356194490192345):
         a=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*t + ph)
         a=np.maximum(a,1e-3)
         out.append(a)
    # products
    for aa in (0.0,0.5,1.0):
      for bb in (0.0,0.5,1.0):
         a=(x+1.0)**aa * (n - x + 1.0)**bb
         out.append(a)
    # randoms
    if n <= 512:
       for alpha in (0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0):
          out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
          out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    else:
       for alpha in (0.5,1.0,2.0):
          out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    # random weighted binary
    for dens in (0.3,0.5,0.7):
       a = (rng.random(n) < dens).astype(np.float64)
       out.append(a)
       # weighted by ramp
       out.append(a * (x+1.0))
       if n <= 1024:
          out.append(a * ((x+1.0)**1.5))
    # parent
    if parent is not None:
       if parent.size == n:
          out.append(parent.copy())
       else:
          xi=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,parent.size)
          xo=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
          pp=np.interp(xo, xi, parent)
          out.append(pp)
          # smooth
          k=max(3, int(round(n/32)))
          kernel=np.ones(k)/float(k)
          sm=np.convolve(pp, kernel, mode='same')
          out.append(sm)
          out.append(pp * (0.5 + t))
          out.append(pp * (0.5 + (1.0 - t)))
          out.append(sm * (0.5 + t))
          out.append(sm * (0.5 + (1.0 - t)))
    # filter normalize
    res=[]
    for a in out:
       na=normalize_array(a)
       if na is not None:
          res.append(na)
    return res
```
This may generate for n=2048 product loops 9*2 etc, okay. Using `a[::-1].copy()` okay.

We should be careful `np.interp` if parent.size <2. If parent size 1, handle: `pp=np.full(n, float(parent[0]))`. Add check.

Now, `optimize_sequence` implementation in detail:
```
def optimize_sequence(a0, time_limit, allow_lp):
    a = normalize_array(a0)
    if a is None:
       a = np.ones(128)
       a /= a.sum()
    n = a.size
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_array(best_a)
    update_best(best_a)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(time_limit))
    power_settings = [(1.0,0.15), (3.0,0.08), (8.0,0.04), (20.0,0.02), (50.0,0.01)]
    idx=0
    no_improve=0
    lp_used=0
    last_lp_time=0.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        c = fft_conv(a)
        current = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
        if current < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best_sc = current; best_a = a.copy(); update_best(best_a); no_improve=0
        advanced=False
        # Try mirror setting
        power, frac = power_settings[idx]
        cand, sc = mirror_step_from_c(a, c, current, power, frac)
        if cand is not None and sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
            a = cand; best_a = a.copy(); best_sc = sc; update_best(best_a); no_improve=0; advanced=True
        elif cand is not None and sc < current + 1e-9 and no_improve > 20:
            # accept almost to move
            a = cand; no_improve = 0? maybe not; advanced=True
            # do not update best
        else:
            no_improve += 1
        if not advanced:
            # maybe try next setting in same outer? To reduce iterations, if no_improve <3:
            if no_improve <= 3:
                idx = (idx + 1) % len(power_settings)
                # try one more mirror with new setting without recomputing c? c still current
                power, frac = power_settings[idx]
                cand, sc = mirror_step_from_c(a, c, current, power, frac)
                if cand is not None and sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                    a=cand; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; update_best(best_a); no_improve=0; advanced=True
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
        if not advanced and allow_lp and n <= 512 and time.monotonic() > last_lp_time + 3.0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0:
            cand = lp_candidate(a)
            last_lp_time = time.monotonic()
            if cand is not None:
                sc = score_array(cand)
                if sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                    a=cand; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; update_best(best_a); no_improve=0; advanced=True
                    lp_used +=1
        if not advanced:
            # after several no-improve, random perturb from best
            if no_improve > 40:
                base = best_a.copy()
                noise = 1.0 + 0.2 * RNG.standard_normal(base.size)
                pert = base * np.maximum(noise, 0.2)
                pert = normalize_array(pert)
                if pert is not None:
                    a = pert
                no_improve = 0
                idx = 0
                power_settings = [(1.0,0.15), (3.0,0.08), (8.0,0.04), (20.0,0.02), (50.0,0.01)]  # reassign? Local okay
            else:
                idx = (idx + 1) % len(power_settings)
        # If best_sc extremely low break
        if best_sc < 1.01:
            break
    return best_a, best_sc
```

Sort/prune: Define:
```
def result_sort_key(item): return item[0]
def prune_results(results, max_jobs, max_per_len):
    results.sort(key=result_sort_key)
    jobs=[]; counts={}
    for item in results:
       sc,n,a=item
       if len(jobs)>=max_jobs: break
       cnt=counts.get(n,0)
       if cnt >= max_per_len: continue
       jobs.append(item)
       counts[n]=cnt+1
    return jobs
```

Let's refine LP base building to avoid Python memory blow. Use list of arrays per m and concatenate:
```
def get_lp_base(n):
    if n in LP_CACHE: return LP_CACHE[n]
    L=2*n-1
    rows_parts=[]; cols_parts=[]; idx_parts=[]
    for m in range(L):
       j0 = max(0, m-n+1)
       j1 = min(n-1, m)
       js = np.arange(j0, j1+1, dtype=np.int64)
       if js.size:
          rows_parts.append(np.full(js.shape, m, dtype=np.int64))
          cols_parts.append(js)
          idx_parts.append((m - js).astype(np.int32))
    rows=np.concatenate(rows_parts) if rows_parts else np.empty(0,dtype=np.int64)
    cols=np.concatenate(cols_parts) if cols_parts else np.empty(0,dtype=np.int64)
    idx=np.concatenate(idx_parts) if idx_parts else np.empty(0,dtype=np.int32)
    LP_CACHE[n]=(rows,cols,idx)
    return ...
```
This loops L but uses vector per row; good. For n=512, 1023 rows, fine. For n=1024, 2047 rows. Good.

```
def lbfgs_polish(a0, time_limit):
   a=normalize_array(a0)
   if a is None: return a0, score_array(a0)
   best=a; best_sc=score_array(best)
   deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
   for lam in (10.0, 25.0, 60.0, 120.0):
      if time.monotonic() > deadline-5: break
      n=a.size
      x0=np.log(a+1e-12)
      x0 -= x0.mean()
      # maxfun decreasing
      maxfun = 200 if n<=128 else (80 if n<=256 else 40)
      # Use minimize with options maxiter max(20, maxfun//2)
      try:
        res=minimize(smooth_fun, x0, args=(lam,), jac=smooth_grad, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=[(-50,50)]*n, options={'maxiter': max(20, maxfun//5), 'maxfun': maxfun, 'maxls':20, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-10, 'disp':False})
      except Exception: res=None
      if res is not None and hasattr(res,'x'):
         aa=softmax_array(res.x)
         sc=score_array(aa)
         if sc < best_sc:
             best=aa; best_sc=sc; update_best(best)
   return best,best_sc
```

`fft_conv`:
```
def fft_conv(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2*n - 1
    m = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, m)[:size]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c
```
If size=1, bit_length? (0).bit_length? size-1=0 -> 1<<0=1. ok.

`normalize_array`:
```
def normalize_array(a):
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
       return None
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr=arr.clip(0.0, 1000.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14: return None
    arr=arr/s
    return arr
```
Clipping to 1000 before normalize might change if original has >1000; but evaluator clamps to1000 then no scaling invariant? Actually if values >1000 and others small, clipping changes scale. Our sequences ≤1, ok. For parent maybe huge; clipping before normalize may alter intended if parent had >1000. Evaluator clamps, so matching evaluator: clip to1000 before normalization. But score invariant if all clipped? If some clipped, relative changes. We should mimic evaluator: after nonneg, clip to 1000, then use sum after clipping. Our normalize after clipping. Good.

But if a length 128 normalized sum1, clipping no effect.

`score_array`:
```
def score_array(a):
    arr=normalize_array(a)
    if arr is None: return float('inf')
    n=arr.size
    c=fft_conv(arr)
    if c.size==0: return inf
    M=float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(M): return inf
    return float(2.0*n*M)
```

`update_best`:
```
def update_best(a):
    global BEST_SCORE, BEST_SEQ
    arr=normalize_array(a)
    if arr is None: return
    sc=score_array(arr)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < BEST_SCORE - 1e-12:
       BEST_SCORE=sc; BEST_SEQ=arr.copy()
```

`make_weights`:
```
def make_weights(c, power, frac, min_active=5):
    L=c.size
    if L==0: return np.array([])
    M=float(c.max())
    if M <= 0: 
       z=np.zeros(L); z[0]=1; return z
    w=np.zeros(L,dtype=np.float64)
    thresh = M * (1.0 - frac)
    active = c > thresh
    if int(np.count_nonzero(active)) < min_active:
       idx=np.argsort(c)[-min_active:]
       active[idx]=True
    vals = c[active] / M
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 1e-6)
    if power != 1.0:
       w[active] = vals ** power
    else:
       w[active] = vals
    sw=w.sum()
    if sw <= 0 or not np.isfinite(sw):
       w=np.zeros(L); w[np.argmax(c)]=1.0
    else:
       w /= sw
    return w
```

`gradient_from_weights`:
```
def gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    n=a.size
    g=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float64)
    if w.size != 2*n-1: return g
    # threshold
    wm_max = float(w.max())
    thresh = wm_max * 1e-10
    if thresh <=0: return g
    for m_int in np.nonzero(w > thresh)[0]:
       m=int(m_int)
       wm=w[m]
       if wm == 0: continue
       j0 = max(0, m - n + 1)
       j1 = min(n - 1, m)
       if j0 > j1: continue
       start = m - j1
       end = m - j0
       # a slice reversed
       seg = a[start:end+1]
       g[j0:j1+1] += wm * seg[::-1]
    return g
```
Check if `seg[::-1]` length matches j1-j0+1. start=m-j1, end=m-j0. length=end-start+1 = (m-j0)-(m-j1)+1=j1-j0+1. Good. For m=0, start0,end0.

`mirror_step_from_c`:
```
def mirror_step_from_c(a, c, current_score, power, frac):
    min_active = max(3, int(0.02 * (2*a.size-1))) if a.size>1 else 3
    w=make_weights(c, power, frac, min_active)
    g=gradient_from_weights(a,w)
    mean_g=float(np.mean(g))
    g = g - mean_g
    std_g=float(np.std(g))
    if std_g <= 1e-13:
       return None, current_score
    g = g / std_g
    best_cand=None
    best_sc=current_score
    for eta in ETA_STEPS:
       lf = -eta * g
       mx=float(np.max(lf))
       if not np.isfinite(mx): continue
       lf = lf - mx
       cand = a * np.exp(lf)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(cand)): continue
       cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-15)
       s=cand.sum()
       if s < 1e-12: continue
       cand = cand / s
       sc = score_array(cand)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
          best_sc = sc
          best_cand = cand.copy()
    return best_cand, best_sc
```
If `lf` all very negative, cand underflows to zeros, floor restores 1e-15, nearly uniform? That might be bad but score checked. Good.

`softmax_array`:
```
def softmax_array(x):
   x=np.asarray(x,float).reshape(-1)
   z=x - float(np.max(x))
   e=np.exp(z)
   s=e.sum()
   if s <=0 or not finite: return None
   return e/s
```

`smooth_fun`:
```
def smooth_fun(x, lam):
   a=softmax_array(x)
   if a is None: return 1e100
   c=fft_conv(a)
   z=lam*c
   zmax=float(np.max(z))
   if not finite: return 1e100
   z=z-zmax
   s=float(np.sum(np.exp(z)))
   if s<=0 or not finite: return 1e100
   lse=zmax + math.log(s)
   n=a.size
   return float(2.0 * n * lse / lam)
```
`smooth_grad`:
```
def smooth_grad(x, lam):
   a=softmax_array(x)
   n=a.size
   c=fft_conv(a)
   z=lam*c; zmax=float(np.max(z)); z=z-zmax
   ez=np.exp(z); s=ez.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: return np.zeros(n)
   w=ez/s
   G=2.0 * gradient_from_weights(a,w)
   dot=float(np.dot(a,G))
   grad = 2.0 * n * (a * (G - dot))
   grad[~np.isfinite(grad)] = 0.0
   return grad
```
Note: `gradient_from_weights` loops all active (w positive); for smooth_grad, to speed we can lower threshold? It uses w.max()*1e-10; all positive maybe many. ok.

`lbfgs_polish`:
```
def lbfgs_polish(a0, time_limit):
   a=normalize_array(a0)
   if a is None: return np.ones(128), 2.0
   best=a.copy(); best_sc=score_array(best)
   update_best(best)
   deadline=time.monotonic()+max(5.0,float(time_limit))
   n=a.size
   if not HAVE_SCIPY or n > 512: return best,best_sc
   lams=(10.0,25.0,60.0,120.0)
   for lam in lams:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0: break
       x0=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-12))
       x0 -= float(np.mean(x0))
       if n <=128: maxfun=250; maxiter=60
       elif n<=256: maxfun=150; maxiter=40
       else: maxfun=70; maxiter=20
       bounds=[(-50.0,50.0) for _ in range(n)]
       try:
          res=minimize(smooth_fun, x0, args=(lam,), jac=smooth_grad, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'maxfun': maxfun, 'maxls':20, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-10, 'disp':False})
       except Exception:
          res=None
       if res is not None and hasattr(res,'x'):
          aa=softmax_array(res.x)
          if aa is not None:
             sc=score_array(aa)
             if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best=aa.copy(); best_sc=sc; update_best(best)
   return best,best_sc
```
List comprehension `for _ in range(n)` not lambda. ok.

`get_lp_base` as above.

`lp_candidate` as above. Could be large code.

`optimize_sequence`:
```
def optimize_sequence(a0, time_limit, allow_lp=True):
   a=normalize_array(a0)
   if a is None:
       a=np.ones(128); a/=a.sum()
   n=a.size
   best_a=a.copy()
   best_sc=score_array(best_a)
   update_best(best_a)
   deadline=time.monotonic()+max(1.0,float(time_limit))
   settings=((1.0,0.15),(3.0,0.08),(8.0,0.04),(20.0,0.02),(50.0,0.01))
   idx=0; no_improve=0; last_lp=0.0; change=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
      c=fft_conv(a)
      current=float(2.0*n*float(c.max()))
      if current < best_sc - 1e-12:
          best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=current; update_best(best_a); no_improve=0
      advanced=False
      power, frac=settings[idx]
      min_active=max(3, int(0.02*(2*n-1)))
      cand, sc = mirror_step_from_c(a,c,current,power,frac)
      if cand is not None and sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
          a=cand; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; update_best(best_a); no_improve=0; advanced=True
      else:
          no_improve += 1
      if not advanced and no_improve >= 4 and allow_lp and n <= 512 and time.monotonic() > last_lp + 4.0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0:
          cand = lp_candidate(a)
          last_lp = time.monotonic()
          if cand is not None:
              sc = score_array(cand)
              if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                  a=cand; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; update_best(best_a); no_improve=0; advanced=True
      if not advanced:
          no_improve += 1
      if not advanced:
          if no_improve <= 4:
              idx = (idx + 1) % len(settings)
          elif no_improve > 50:
              # random perturb from best
              base=best_a.copy()
              noise=1.0 + 0.25*RNG.standard_normal(base.size)
              pert=base*np.maximum(noise,0.1)
              pert=normalize_array(pert)
              if pert is not None:
                  a=pert
              no_improve=0
              idx=0
          else:
              idx=(idx+1)%len(settings)
      if best_sc < 1.01:
          break
   return best_a, best_sc
```

Potential double increment of no_improve; not critical.

We should if `sc < current` even if not global best, to make progress from current. But line search chooses best eta relative to current; `best_sc` initialized current, so returns improvement over current. If current > global best (after random), improving current to still above global best should be accepted to climb. Our condition `sc < best_sc` (global) would reject, causing no progress from perturbation. Better accept if `sc < current - tol`, and also update global if `sc < best_sc`. Modify:
```
if cand is not None and sc < current - 1e-10:
    a=cand
    if sc < best_sc - 1e-10: update best; no_improve=0
    else: no_improve=0? reset? maybe if improved from current, reset no_improve half? Set no_improve=0 to allow continue.
    advanced=True
```

`generate_arrays` with parent interpolation: Need top-level `downsample_parent(parent, n)`.
```
def interpolate_parent(parent, n):
    parent = np.asarray(parent,float)
    if parent.size == 0: return None
    if parent.size == n: return parent.copy()
    clamped=np.clip(parent,0,1000)
    s=clamped.sum()
    if s>0: clamped=clamped/s # maybe not; interpolation of normalized.
    if parent.size == 1:
       return np.full(n, float(clamped[0]))
    xi=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,parent.size)
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    return np.interp(xo, xi, clamped)
```
Use in generate.

`generate_arrays`:
```
def generate_arrays(n, rng, parent):
    out=[]
    if n <= 0: return out
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
    denom = float(max(1, n-1))
    t = x / denom
    # uniform
    out.append(np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64))
    # powers
    for p in (0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0,1.25,1.5,2.0):
       a=(x+1.0)**p
       out.append(a)
       out.append(a[::-1].copy())
    # exp
    for b in (0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0):
       a=np.exp(b*t)
       out.append(a)
       out.append(a[::-1].copy())
    # cos
    amp_list=(0.2,0.5,0.8)
    ph_list=(0.0,0.7853981633974483,1.5707963267948966,2.356194490192345)
    for amp in amp_list:
       for ph in ph_list:
          a=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*t + ph)
          a=np.maximum(a,1e-3)
          out.append(a)
    # products
    for aa in (0.0,0.5,1.0):
       for bb in (0.0,0.5,1.0):
          a=(x+1.0)**aa * (n - x + 1.0)**bb
          out.append(a)
    # random
    if n <= 512:
       for alpha in (0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0):
           out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
           out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    else:
       for alpha in (0.5,1.0,2.0):
           out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    for dens in (0.3,0.5,0.7):
       a=(rng.random(n) < dens).astype(np.float64)
       out.append(a)
       out.append(a * (x+1.0))
       if n <= 1024:
          out.append(a * ((x+1.0)**1.5))
    if parent is not None:
       pp=interpolate_parent(parent, n)
       if pp is not None:
          out.append(pp)
          k=max(3, int(round(n/32)))
          kernel=np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) / float(k)
          sm=np.convolve(pp, kernel, mode='same')
          out.append(sm)
          out.append(pp * (0.5 + t))
          out.append(pp * (0.5 + (1.0 - t)))
          out.append(sm * (0.5 + t))
          out.append(sm * (0.5 + (1.0 - t)))
          # noised parent
          noise=1.0 + 0.1*rng.standard_normal(n)
          out.append(pp * np.maximum(noise,0.5))
    # filter normalize
    res=[]
    for a in out:
       na=normalize_array(a)
       if na is not None and na.size == n:
          res.append(na)
    return res
```
This for n=2048 includes cos 12, powers14, exp8, products9, random3, binary6, parent6 ~58 arrays. 6 lengths ~300, fine. But for each n, random Dirichlet n=2048 3 times ~ OK.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(linear_conv(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2.

Strategy
--------
1. Generate many deterministic/normalised starting shapes (ramps, powers,
   exponentials, cosine masks, random Dirichlet/binary, parent-derived).
2. Keep the best and a diverse set of promising jobs by length.
3. Polish each job using:
   - entropy/mirror descent on the probability simplex with active-peak
     subgradients and line search over multiplicative updates;
   - occasional LP-direction steps: choose y >= 0, sum y = 1, minimising
     max_m (a*y)_m, then line-search convex blends;
   - optional L-BFGS-B optimisation of a log-sum-exp smoothed objective.
4. At the end, use the provided evaluate_sequence to choose the final list.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog, minimize
    from scipy import sparse
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


RNG = np.random.default_rng(42)

BEST_SCORE = float("inf")
BEST_SEQ = None
CAND_STORE = []
LP_CACHE = {}

ETA_STEPS = (0.003, 0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 1.0, 3.0)


def fft_conv(a):
    """Fast linear auto-convolution using FFT."""
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    m = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, m)[:size]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def normalize_array(a):
    """Clip to evaluator-safe range, then normalise to sum 1."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = arr.clip(0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14:
        return None
    arr = arr / s
    return arr


def score_array(a):
    """Internal score equivalent to evaluate_sequence for valid arrays."""
    arr = normalize_array(a)
    if arr is None:
        return float("inf")
    n = arr.size
    c = fft_conv(arr)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    M = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * M)


def update_best(a):
    """Update global best according to internal score."""
    global BEST_SCORE, BEST_SEQ
    arr = normalize_array(a)
    if arr is None:
        return
    sc = score_array(arr)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < BEST_SCORE - 1e-12:
        BEST_SCORE = sc
        BEST_SEQ = arr.copy()


def make_weights(c, power, frac, min_active=5):
    """Soft weights over convolution coefficients near their peak."""
    L = int(c.size)
    if L == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    M = float(c.max())
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        z = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        z[0] = 1.0
        return z

    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    thresh = M * (1.0 - frac)
    active = c > thresh
    if int(np.count_nonzero(active)) < min_active:
        idx = np.argsort(c)[-min_active:]
        active[idx] = True

    vals = c[active] / M
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 1e-6)
    if power != 1.0:
        w[active] = vals ** power
    else:
        w[active] = vals

    sw = float(w.sum())
    if sw <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(sw):
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= sw
    return w


def gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    """
    Gradient (up to a constant factor) of sum_m w_m conv_m(a,a)
    with respect to a on the full probability vector.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    if w.size != 2 * n - 1:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    wm_max = float(w.max())
    if wm_max <= 0.0:
        return g
    thresh = wm_max * 1e-10
    for m_int in np.nonzero(w > thresh)[0]:
        m = int(m_int)
        wm = float(w[m])
        if wm == 0.0:
            continue
        j0 = max(0, m - n + 1)
        j1 = min(n - 1, m)
        if j0 > j1:
            continue
        start = m - j1
        end = m - j0
        seg = a[start:end + 1]
        g[j0:j1 + 1] += wm * seg[::-1]
    return g


def mirror_step_from_c(a, c, current_score, power, frac):
    """One mirror-descent line-search step from already-computed convolution."""
    min_active = max(3, int(0.02 * (2 * a.size - 1)))
    w = make_weights(c, power, frac, min_active)
    g = gradient_from_weights(a, w)
    g = g - float(np.mean(g))
    std_g = float(np.std(g))
    if std_g <= 1e-13:
        return None, current_score
    g = g / std_g

    best_cand = None
    best_sc = float(current_score)

    for eta in ETA_STEPS:
        lf = -eta * g
        mx = float(np.max(lf))
        if not np.isfinite(mx):
            continue
        lf = lf - mx
        cand = a * np.exp(lf)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(cand)):
            continue
        cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-15)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s < 1e-12:
            continue
        cand = cand / s
        sc = score_array(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_cand = cand.copy()

    return best_cand, best_sc


def softmax_array(x):
    """Numerically stable softmax to probability vector."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if x.size == 0:
        return None
    z = x - float(np.max(x))
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return e / s


def smooth_fun(x, lam):
    """Smoothed objective: 2*n*LogSumExp(lam*conv)/lam."""
    a = softmax_array(x)
    if a is None:
        return 1e100
    c = fft_conv(a)
    z = lam * c
    zmax = float(np.max(z))
    if not np.isfinite(zmax):
        return 1e100
    z = z - zmax
    s = float(np.sum(np.exp(z)))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return 1e100
    lse = zmax + math.log(s)
    n = a.size
    return float(2.0 * n * lse / lam)


def smooth_grad(x, lam):
    """Gradient of smooth_fun with respect to log-softmax variables."""
    a = softmax_array(x)
    n = a.size
    c = fft_conv(a)
    z = lam * c
    zmax = float(np.max(z))
    z = z - zmax
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s = float(ez.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    w = ez / s
    G = 2.0 * gradient_from_weights(a, w)
    dot = float(np.dot(a, G))
    grad = 2.0 * n * (a * (G - dot))
    grad[~np.isfinite(grad)] = 0.0
    return grad


def lbfgs_polish(a0, time_limit):
    """Polish a candidate using L-BFGS-B on log-sum-exp objective."""
    a = normalize_array(a0)
    if a is None:
        a = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64)
        a /= a.sum()
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_array(best)
    update_best(best)

    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return best, best_sc

    n = a.size
    if n > 512:
        return best, best_sc

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(5.0, float(time_limit))
    lams = (10.0, 25.0, 60.0, 120.0)

    for lam in lams:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break
        x0 = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))
        x0 -= float(np.mean(x0))

        if n <= 128:
            maxfun = 260
            maxiter = 70
        elif n <= 256:
            maxfun = 160
            maxiter = 45
        else:
            maxfun = 80
            maxiter = 25

        bounds = [(-50.0, 50.0) for _ in range(n)]
        try:
            res = minimize(
                smooth_fun,
                x0,
                args=(lam,),
                jac=smooth_grad,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                bounds=bounds,
                options={
                    "maxiter": maxiter,
                    "maxfun": maxfun,
                    "maxls": 20,
                    "ftol": 1e-12,
                    "gtol": 1e-10,
                    "disp": False,
                },
            )
        except Exception:
            res = None

        if res is not None and hasattr(res, "x"):
            aa = softmax_array(res.x)
            if aa is not None:
                sc = score_array(aa)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                    best = aa.copy()
                    best_sc = sc
                    update_best(best)

    return best, best_sc


def get_lp_base(n):
    """Cache indices for LP matrix A_y whose rows are convolution windows."""
    if n in LP_CACHE:
        return LP_CACHE[n]
    L = 2 * n - 1
    rows_parts = []
    cols_parts = []
    idx_parts = []
    for m in range(L):
        j0 = max(0, m - n + 1)
        j1 = min(n - 1, m)
        js = np.arange(j0, j1 + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        if js.size:
            rows_parts.append(np.full(js.shape, m, dtype=np.int64))
            cols_parts.append(js)
            idx_parts.append((m - js).astype(np.int32))
    if rows_parts:
        rows = np.concatenate(rows_parts)
        cols = np.concatenate(cols_parts)
        idx = np.concatenate(idx_parts)
    else:
        rows = np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)
        cols = np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)
        idx = np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32)
    LP_CACHE[n] = (rows, cols, idx)
    return rows, cols, idx


def lp_candidate(a):
    """
    Linear-programming direction:
        min t
        s.t. sum_j a_{m-j} y_j <= t for all m,
             sum y_j = 1,
             y_j >= 0.
    Then line-search blends with a.
    """
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    a = normalize_array(a)
    if a is None:
        return None
    n = a.size
    if n < 32 or n > 512:
        return None

    c = fft_conv(a)
    M = float(c.max())
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None

    try:
        rows, cols, cidx = get_lp_base(n)
        L = 2 * n - 1

        A_y = sparse.csr_matrix(
            (a[cidx], (rows, cols)),
            shape=(L, n),
            dtype=np.float64
        )

        rows_t = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
        data_t = -np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)
        A_t = sparse.csr_matrix(
            (data_t, (rows_t, rows_t)),
            shape=(L, 1),
            dtype=np.float64
        )

        A_ub = sparse.hstack((A_y, A_t), format="csc")
        b_ub = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)

        c_obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
        c_obj[-1] = 1.0

        A_eq = sparse.lil_matrix((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
        A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
        A_eq = A_eq.tocsr()
        b_eq = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

        bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
        bounds.append((0.0, float(M) * (1.0 + 1e-8)))

        res = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A_ub,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            A_eq=A_eq,
            b_eq=b_eq,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"disp": False}
        )

        if res is None or res.status != 0:
            return None

        y = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
        y = np.maximum(y, 0.0)
        s = float(y.sum())
        if s < 1e-8 or not np.isfinite(s):
            return None
        y = y / s

        best_cand = None
        best_sc = score_array(a)
        for lam in (1.0, 0.7, 0.5, 0.3, 0.15, 0.05):
            cand = (1.0 - lam) * a + lam * y
            cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
            ss = float(cand.sum())
            if ss < 1e-12:
                continue
            cand = cand / ss
            sc = score_array(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_cand = cand.copy()
        return best_cand

    except Exception:
        return None


def optimize_sequence(a0, time_limit, allow_lp=True):
    """
    Mirror descent + optional LP directions.
    Returns (best_sequence, best_internal_score).
    """
    a = normalize_array(a0)
    if a is None:
        a = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64)
        a /= a.sum()

    n = a.size
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_array(best_a)
    update_best(best_a)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(time_limit))
    settings = (
        (1.0, 0.15),
        (3.0, 0.08),
        (8.0, 0.04),
        (20.0, 0.02),
        (50.0, 0.01),
    )
    idx = 0
    no_improve = 0
    last_lp = 0.0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        c = fft_conv(a)
        current = float(2.0 * n * float(c.max()))

        if current < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_sc = current
            update_best(best_a)
            no_improve = 0

        advanced = False
        power, frac = settings[idx]
        cand, sc = mirror_step_from_c(a, c, current, power, frac)

        if cand is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current - 1e-10:
            a = cand
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_sc = sc
                update_best(best_a)
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                if no_improve > 0:
                    no_improve = max(0, no_improve - 2)
            advanced = True
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if (
            not advanced
            and no_improve >= 4
            and allow_lp
            and n <= 512
            and time.monotonic() > last_lp + 5.0
            and time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0
        ):
            cand = lp_candidate(a)
            last_lp = time.monotonic()
            if cand is not None:
                sc = score_array(cand)
                if cand is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current - 1e-10:
                    a = cand
                    if sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                        best_a = a.copy()
                        best_sc = sc
                        update_best(best_a)
                    no_improve = 0
                    advanced = True

        if not advanced:
            no_improve += 1

        if not advanced:
            if no_improve <= 4:
                idx = (idx + 1) % len(settings)
            elif no_improve > 50:
                base = best_a.copy()
                noise = 1.0 + 0.25 * RNG.standard_normal(base.size)
                pert = base * np.maximum(noise, 0.1)
                pert = normalize_array(pert)
                if pert is not None:
                    a = pert
                no_improve = 0
                idx = 0
            else:
                idx = (idx + 1) % len(settings)

        if best_sc < 1.01:
            break

    return best_a, best_sc


def interpolate_parent(parent, n):
    """Interpolate a parent sequence to length n, clipped/normalised-style."""
    p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = p.clip(0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    p = p / s

    if p.size == n:
        return p.copy()
    if p.size == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(p[0]), dtype=np.float64)

    xi = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size)
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return np.interp(xo, xi, p)


def generate_arrays(n, rng, parent):
    """Generate diverse initial non-negative sequences of length n."""
    out = []
    if n <= 0:
        return out

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    denom = float(max(1, n - 1))
    t = x / denom

    # Basic deterministic shapes.
    out.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    for p in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0):
        a = (x + 1.0) ** p
        out.append(a)
        out.append(a[::-1].copy())

    for b in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0):
        a = np.exp(b * t)
        out.append(a)
        out.append(a[::-1].copy())

    amps = (0.2, 0.5, 0.8)
    phases = (0.0, 0.7853981633974483, 1.5707963267948966, 2.356194490192345)
    for amp in amps:
        for ph in phases:
            a = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * t + ph)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-3)
            out.append(a)

    # Two-ended beta-like products.
    for aa in (0.0, 0.5, 1.0):
        for bb in (0.0, 0.5, 1.0):
            a = (x + 1.0) ** aa * (n - x + 1.0) ** bb
            out.append(a)

    # Random smooth shapes.
    if n <= 512:
        for alpha in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0):
            out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
            out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    else:
        for alpha in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
            out.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))

    # Sparse/weighted binary shapes.
    for dens in (0.3, 0.5, 0.7):
        b = (rng.random(n) < dens).astype(np.float64)
        out.append(b)
        out.append(b * (x + 1.0))
        if n <= 1024:
            out.append(b * ((x + 1.0) ** 1.5))

    # Parent-derived shapes.
    if parent is not None:
        pp = interpolate_parent(parent, n)
        if pp is not None:
            out.append(pp)
            k = max(3, int(round(n / 32)))
            kernel = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) / float(k)
            sm = np.convolve(pp, kernel, mode="same")
            out.append(sm)
            out.append(pp * (0.5 + t))
            out.append(pp * (0.5 + (1.0 - t)))
            out.append(sm * (0.5 + t))
            out.append(sm * (0.5 + (1.0 - t)))
            noise = 1.0 + 0.1 * rng.standard_normal(n)
            out.append(pp * np.maximum(noise, 0.5))

    res = []
    for a in out:
        na = normalize_array(a)
        if na is not None and na.size == n:
            res.append(na)
    return res


def result_key(item):
    return item[0]


def prune_results(results, max_jobs, max_per_len):
    """Sort by score and keep diverse lengths."""
    results.sort(key=result_key)
    jobs = []
    counts = {}
    for item in results:
        if len(jobs) >= max_jobs:
            break
        sc, n, a = item
        cnt = counts.get(n, 0)
        if cnt >= max_per_len:
            continue
        jobs.append(item)
        counts[n] = cnt + 1
    return jobs


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2380, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.
    Runs the hybrid search and returns a list of non-negative floats.
    """
    global BEST_SCORE, BEST_SEQ, CAND_STORE

    t_start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2380.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 30.0:
        budget = 2380.0
    deadline = t_start + max(30.0, budget - 20.0)

    BEST_SCORE = float("inf")
    BEST_SEQ = None
    CAND_STORE = []

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) % (2 ** 31))

    # Fallback: uniform.
    update_best(np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64))

    # Parent sequence, if present.
    parent = None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            p = p.clip(0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(p.sum())
            if s > 1e-12:
                parent = p / s
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    lengths = [64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048]
    if parent is not None and 32 <= parent.size <= 2048:
        lengths.append(parent.size)
    lengths = sorted(set(lengths))

    print(f"Starting search; deadline in {deadline - t_start:.1f}s")

    results = []
    scan_deadline = t_start + min(180.0, max(20.0, 0.08 * (deadline - t_start)))

    for n in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > scan_deadline:
            break
        arrs = generate_arrays(n, rng, parent)
        for a in arrs:
            sc = score_array(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                results.append((sc, n, a))
                update_best(a)
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 90.0:
            break

    print(f"After scan: best_internal={BEST_SCORE:.6f}, candidates={len(results)}")

    jobs = prune_results(results, max_jobs=16, max_per_len=3)
    if not jobs:
        u = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64)
        u /= u.sum()
        jobs = [(score_array(u), 128, u)]
        update_best(u)

    # Allocate time to promising jobs.
    final_reserve = 70.0
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - final_reserve
    if remaining > 0.0:
        njobs = max(1, len(jobs))
        time_per = remaining / float(njobs)

        for job_idx, item in enumerate(jobs):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - final_reserve:
                break

            sc0, n, a0 = item
            t_alloc = min(max(8.0, time_per), max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - final_reserve))
            if t_alloc < 8.0:
                continue

            t_mirror = max(5.0, 0.68 * t_alloc)
            t_lbg = max(0.0, t_alloc - t_mirror - 2.0)

            allow_lp = (n <= 512) and (t_mirror > 20.0)
            t0 = time.monotonic()
            a_best, sc1 = optimize_sequence(a0, t_mirror, allow_lp=allow_lp)

            if t_lbg > 12.0 and a_best is not None and a_best.size <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline - final_reserve - 5.0:
                a2, sc2 = lbfgs_polish(a_best, t_lbg)
                if a2 is not None and sc2 < sc1:
                    a_best = a2
                    sc1 = sc2

            if a_best is not None:
                arr = normalize_array(a_best)
                if arr is not None and len(CAND_STORE) < 24:
                    CAND_STORE.append(arr.copy())
                print(
                    f"Job {job_idx+1}/{len(jobs)} n={n} "
                    f"init={sc0:.6f} best={sc1:.6f} "
                    f"elapsed={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s"
                )

    # Final selection using the official evaluator when available.
    final_candidates = list(CAND_STORE)
    if BEST_SEQ is not None:
        final_candidates.append(BEST_SEQ.copy())

    final_seq = None
    final_score = float("inf")

    for a in final_candidates:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 3.0:
            break
        arr = normalize_array(a)
        if arr is None:
            continue
        try:
            vals = [float(v) for v in arr.tolist()]
            if not vals:
                continue
            sc = float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
        except Exception:
            continue
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < final_score:
            final_score = sc
            final_seq = vals

    if final_seq is None or len(final_seq) == 0:
        if BEST_SEQ is not None:
            arr = normalize_array(BEST_SEQ)
            if arr is not None:
                final_seq = [float(v) for v in arr.tolist()]
        if final_seq is None or len(final_seq) == 0:
            u = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64)
            u /= u.sum()
            final_seq = [float(v) for v in u.tolist()]

    out = []
    for v in final_seq:
        if not np.isfinite(v):
            v = 0.0
        if v < 0.0:
            v = 0.0
        if v > 1000.0:
            v = 1000.0
        out.append(float(v))

    print(f"FINAL local best: {BEST_SCORE:.6f}")
    if np.isfinite(final_score):
        print(f"FINAL evaluated best: {final_score:.6f}")
    print(f"Final length: {len(out)}")

    return out
```
